\int_{\mathbb{R}+i\frac{\pi}{4}}F(z)dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t+i\frac{\pi}{4}}{\sinh(t+i\frac{\pi}{4})}e^{2i\omega(t+i\frac{\pi}{4}-\nu^{2}\tanh(t+i\frac{\pi}{4}))}dt\\
=e^{-\pi\omega/2}2\sqrt{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t+i\frac{\pi}{4}}{(1+i)e^{t}-(1-i)e^{-t}}e^{2i\omega t}e^{-2i\omega\nu^{2}\frac{e^{t}(1+i)-e^{-t}(1-i)}{e^{t}(1+i)+e^{-t}(1-i)}}dt\\
=e^{-\pi\omega/2}\sqrt{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t+i\frac{\pi}{4}}{\sinh t+i\cosh t}e^{2i\omega t}e^{-2i\omega\nu^{2}\frac{e^{t}(1+i)-e^{-t}(1-i)}{e^{t}(1+i)+e^{-t}(1-i)}}dt.

We have

\operatorname{Im}\left(\frac{e^{t}(1+i)-e^{-t}(1-i)}{e^{t}(1+i)+e^{-t}(1-i)}\right)=\operatorname{Im}\left(\frac{\sinh t+i\cosh t}{\cosh t+i\sinh t}\right)=\frac{1}{\cosh^{2}t+\sinh^{2}t},

so that

(9.6.6)

\Bigl|{\int_{\mathbb{R}+i\frac{\pi}{4}}F(z)dz}\Bigr|\leq e^{-\frac{\pi\omega}{2}}\sqrt{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sqrt{t^{2}+(\frac{\pi}{4})^{2}}}{\sqrt{\sinh^{2}t+\cosh^{2}t}}\ e^{\frac{2\omega\nu^{2}}{\sinh^{2}t+\cosh^{2}t}}dt\\
=e^{-\frac{\pi\omega}{2}}\sqrt{2}e^{2\kappa}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sqrt{t^{2}+(\frac{\pi}{4})^{2}}}{\sqrt{\sinh^{2}t+\cosh^{2}t}}dt\leq 6e^{-\frac{\pi\omega}{2}}e^{2\kappa}.

Claim 9.32
.

We have

\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\oint_{[R,R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz=\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\oint_{[-R,-R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz=0.

Proof of the Claim.

We note first that

\oint_{[-R,-R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz=-\overline{\oint_{[R,R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz},

so that it is enough to prove one equality.
Indeed for
R>0
, we have

\oint_{[R,R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz=\int_{0}^{\pi/4}\frac{R+it}{\sinh(R+it)}e^{2i\omega(R+it-\nu^{2}\tanh(R+it))}idt,

so that

\Bigl|{\oint_{[R,R+i\pi/4]}F(z)dz}\Bigr|\leq\int_{0}^{\pi/4}\frac{2\sqrt{R^{2}+t^{2}}}{|e^{R+it}||1-e^{-2R-2it}|}e^{-2\omega t}e^{2\kappa\operatorname{Im}(\tanh(R+it))}dt\\
\leq e^{-R}\frac{\sqrt{4R^{2}+\pi^{2}/4}}{{1-e^{-2R}}}\int_{0}^{\pi/4}e^{2\kappa|\frac{1-e^{-2R-2it}}{1+e^{-2R-2it}}|}dt\leq e^{-R}\frac{\sqrt{4R^{2}+\pi^{2}/4}}{{1-e^{-2R}}}\frac{\pi}{4}e^{\frac{4\kappa}{(1-e^{-2R})}},

proving the claim.
∎

Lemma 9.33
.

We have for
\tau>0,\sigma\geq 0
,
\rho_{\sigma}
given in (
5.2.14
),

(9.6.7)

|\rho_{\sigma}(\tau)|\leq 6e^{-{\pi^{2}\tau}}e^{4\pi\sigma}.

Proof.

We have, with the notations (
9.6.2
),
F
given in (
9.6.4
) and

\gamma_{R}=[-R,-R+i\frac{\pi}{4}]\cup[-R+i\frac{\pi}{4},R+i\frac{\pi}{4}]\cup[R+i\frac{\pi}{4},R]
,

\rho_{\sigma}(\tau)=\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\int_{[-R,R]}F(s)ds=\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\left(\oint_{\gamma_{R}}F(z)dz\right)\underbrace{=}_{\text{Claim \eqref{claim3}}}\oint_{\mathbb{R}+\frac{i\pi}{4}}F(z)dz,

so that (
9.6.6
) implies the lemma.
∎

9.6.3.
On the function
\psi_{\nu}

Let
\nu\in(0,1)
be given. We study first the function
\phi_{\nu}
defined on
[0,\pi/2)
by

(9.6.8)

\phi_{\nu}(s)=s-\nu^{2}\tan s,\quad\text{so that \quad}\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)=1-\nu^{2}(1+\tan^{2}s)=\frac{\cos^{2}s-\nu^{2}}{\cos^{2}s},

so that

(9.6.9)

\begin{array}[]{|c|lcccccc|}\hline\cr s&0&&s_{\nu}&&t_{\nu}&&\frac{\pi}{2}\\
\hline\cr\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)&\text{\footnotesize{$1-\nu^{2}$}}&+&0&-&&-&\\
\hline\cr\phi_{\nu}(s)&0&\nearrow&\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})&\searrow&0&\searrow&-{\infty}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

We have

(9.6.10)

\begin{cases}&\hskip-10.0pts_{\nu}=\arccos\nu=\frac{\pi}{2}-\nu+O(\nu^{3}),\\
&\hskip-10.0pt\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})=\arccos\nu-\nu\sqrt{1-\nu^{2}}=\frac{\pi}{2}-2\nu+O(\nu^{3}),\end{cases}\qquad\text{for}\ \nu\rightarrow 0.

The function
\phi_{\nu}
is concave on
(0,\pi/2)

since we have there

\phi_{\nu}^{\prime\prime}(s)=-\nu^{2}(-2)(\cos s)^{-3}(-\sin s)=-\nu^{2}2(\cos s)^{-3}\sin s\leq 0.

We have defined in (
5.2.58
)

(9.6.11)

\psi_{\nu}(\omega)=\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds.

Let us start with an elementary lemma.

Lemma 9.34
.

Let
\lambda>0
be given. Defining

(9.6.12)

J(\lambda)=e^{-\lambda}\int_{0}^{\lambda}\frac{e^{\sigma}-1}{\sigma}d\sigma,\qquad\text{we have}

(9.6.13)

\displaystyle J(\lambda)=\lambda^{-1}+O(\lambda^{-2}),\quad\lambda\rightarrow+{\infty},

(9.6.14)

\displaystyle\forall\lambda>0,\quad J(\lambda)\geq\lambda^{-1}-\lambda^{-2}.

Proof.

Indeed we have for
\lambda>0
,

(9.6.15)

\lambda J(\lambda)=\lambda e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\int_{0}^{\lambda}\frac{\sigma^{k-1}}{k!}d\sigma=\lambda e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k}}{k!k}=e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}\frac{k+1}{k}\\
=e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}+e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k+1}}{(k+1)!}\frac{1}{k}\\
=e^{-\lambda}(e^{\lambda}-1-\lambda)+\lambda^{-1}\underbrace{\left(e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k+2}}{(k+1)!}\frac{1}{k}\right)}_{R(\lambda)},

with

(9.6.16)

\displaystyle 0\leq R(\lambda)\leq e^{-\lambda}\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\lambda^{k+2}}{(k+2)!}\frac{k+2}{k}\leq e^{-\lambda}(e^{\lambda}-1-\lambda-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2})\times 3=O(1),

so that

\lambda J(\lambda)=e^{-\lambda}(e^{\lambda}-1-\lambda)+\lambda^{-1}O(1)=1+\lambda^{-1}O(1)-(1+\lambda)e^{-\lambda}=1+\lambda^{-1}O(1),

proving (
9.6.13
).
Note also that (
9.6.15
), (
9.6.16
) imply, since
R(\lambda)\geq 0
,

\lambda J(\lambda)\geq 1-e^{-\lambda}(1+\lambda),

so that
J(\lambda)\geq\lambda^{-1}-e^{-\lambda}(1+\lambda^{-1}),

and thus
35
35

35

We leave for the reader to check that for
\lambda>0
,
e^{-\lambda}(1+\lambda^{-1})\leq\lambda^{-2}
, which boils dow to study
q(\lambda)=e^{-\lambda}(\lambda^{2}+\lambda)
reaching its maximum for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, at
\lambda_{0}=(1+\sqrt{5})/{2}

with
q(\lambda_{0})\approx 0.84<1.

the sought result
(
9.6.14
).
∎

Remark 9.35
.

Considering now the function
\varphi_{0}
defined by

(9.6.17)

\varphi_{0}(\omega)=\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{e^{2\omega s}-1}{\sin s}ds,

we find that, for
\omega\geq 0
, using Lemma
9.34
,

\varphi_{0}(\omega)\geq\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{e^{2\omega s}-1}{s}ds=\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi\omega}\frac{e^{\sigma}-1}{\sigma}d\sigma=\frac{1}{2\pi}J(\pi\omega),

so that

(9.6.18)

\varphi_{0}(\omega)\geq\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}\omega}-\frac{1}{2\pi^{3}\omega^{2}}.

It is our goal now to prove a minoration of the same flavour for the function
(
9.6.11
) defined above.

Assuming
\nu\in(0,1/2)
,
we have

\frac{\pi}{3}<s_{\nu}<t_{\nu}<\frac{\pi}{2}
(
s_{\nu},t_{\nu}
are defined in (
9.6.9
),
\psi_{\nu}
in (
9.6.11
)),

(9.6.19)

\displaystyle 2\pi

\displaystyle e^{\pi\omega}\psi_{\nu}(\omega)=\int_{0}^{t_{\nu}}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds+\int_{t_{\nu}}^{\pi/2}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds

\displaystyle\geq\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t_{\nu}}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds}_{\text{on $(0,t_{\nu}),\ \phi_{\nu}(s)\geq 0$}}-\int_{t_{\nu}}^{\pi/2}\frac{ds}{\sin s}\geq\int_{0}^{s_{\nu}}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds-\int_{\pi/3}^{\pi/2}\frac{ds}{\sin s}

\displaystyle\hskip 175.0pt=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{s_{\nu}}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\sin s}ds}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{on $(0,s_{\nu})$}\\
\phi_{\nu}(s)>0\ \text{and\ }\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)>0\end{subarray}}-\frac{\ln 3}{2}.

Claim 9.36
.

For
s\in(0,\pi/2)
, we have
\phi_{\nu}(s)\geq\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)\sin s
.
Moreover,
for
s\in(0,s_{\nu})
, we have

\frac{1}{\sin s}\geq\frac{\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)}{\phi_{\nu}(s)}
.

Proof of the Claim.

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{\nu}(s)-

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)\sin s=s-\nu^{2}\tan s-\sin s+\nu^{2}(1+\tan^{2}s)\sin s

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}\bigl(\sin s+\sin s\tan^{2}s-\tan s\bigr)+s-\sin s

\displaystyle=\nu^{2}\bigl(\frac{\sin s}{\cos^{2}s}-\frac{\sin s}{\cos s}\bigr)+s-\sin s

(9.6.20)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu^{2}\sin s}{\cos^{2}s}\bigl(1-\cos s\bigr)+s-\sin s\geq 0,\quad\text{for $s\in(0,\pi/2)$}.

The last part of the claim follows from the first part and the fact that
\sin s

and
\phi_{\nu}(s)
are both positive on
(0,s_{\nu})
.
∎

Going back now to (
9.6.19
), we obtain that for
\nu\in(0,1/2)
and
\omega>0
, we have

(9.6.21)

2\pi e^{\pi\omega}\psi_{\nu}(\omega)\geq\int_{0}^{s_{\nu}}\frac{e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s)}-1}{\phi_{\nu}(s)}\phi^{\prime}_{\nu}(s)ds-\frac{\ln 3}{2}\\
=\int_{0}^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})}\frac{e^{\sigma}-1}{\sigma}d\sigma-\frac{\ln 3}{2}=e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})}J(2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu}))-\frac{\ln 3}{2},

so that, using (
9.6.14
), we get

\psi_{\nu}(\omega)\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega}e^{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})}-\frac{1}{(2\omega\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu}))^{2}}\Bigr)-\frac{\ln 3}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega},

and since
\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})=\frac{\pi}{2}-\epsilon_{\nu}
, with
\epsilon_{\nu}\in(0,\pi/2)
,
we find also that
\epsilon_{\nu}
is a concave function
36
36

36

We have from (
9.6.10
),

\epsilon_{\nu}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\nu+\nu\sqrt{1-\nu^{2}},\ \frac{d\epsilon_{\nu}}{d\nu}=2\sqrt{1-\nu^{2}},\ \frac{d^{2}\epsilon_{\nu}}{d\nu^{2}}=-2\nu/\sqrt{1-\nu^{2}}<0,

so that the concavity gives

\frac{\pi}{2}\nu\leq\epsilon_{\nu}\leq 2\nu.

of
\nu\in(0,1)
and

\frac{\pi\nu}{2}\leq\epsilon_{\nu}\leq 2\nu\quad\text{so that }\quad 2\phi_{\nu}(s_{\nu})=\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu}\in[\pi-{4\nu},\pi-\pi\nu],\quad

so that for
\nu\in(0,1/2]
, we have
37
37

37

We know that

\omega(\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu})\geq\omega(\pi-4\nu)\geq\omega(\pi-2)

so that to ensure
\omega(\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu})\geq 4
, it suffices to assume

.

(assuming
\omega>0
),

\psi_{\nu}(\omega)\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega}e^{\omega(\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu})}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\omega(\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu})}-\frac{1}{(\omega(\pi-2\epsilon_{\nu}))^{2}}\Bigr)-\frac{\ln 3}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega},\\
\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-4\nu\omega}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\omega\pi}-\frac{1}{\omega^{2}(\pi-2)^{2}}\Bigr)-\frac{\ln 3}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega},

We recall the notations (
9.6.2
),
so that
\nu=\sqrt{\kappa/\omega}
i.e.

\nu\omega=\sqrt{\kappa\omega}

and we get

(9.6.22)

\forall\omega>0,\quad\psi_{\nu}(\omega)\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-4\sqrt{\kappa\omega}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\pi\omega}-\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\Bigr)-\frac{\ln 3}{2}\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\pi\omega},\ \nu=\sqrt{\kappa/\omega}.

9.6.4.
An explicit expresssion for
a_{11}

According to (
5.2.31
), we have

(9.6.23)

a_{11}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\sin(2\pi t\tau-4\pi\sigma\tanh(t/2))}{\sinh(t/2)}dt.

We have used in Section
5.2
the equivalent expression

a_{11}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}+\hat{T}_{\sigma}(\tau)
, where
T_{\sigma}
is defined in
(
5.2.12
) and we were able to prove the estimate in Lemma
5.18
. It turns out that (
9.6.7
) is not optimal, and it is interesting to give an “explicit” expression for
a_{11}
as displayed in
[
55
]
.

Using the notations (
9.6.2
), we can write (
9.6.23
) as

(9.6.24)

a_{11}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\operatorname{Im}\frac{\exp{i(\omega t-2\kappa\tanh(t/2))}}{\sinh(t/2)}dt\\
=\frac{1}{2}+\operatorname{Im}\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[-R,R]}\frac{\exp{2i(\omega s-\kappa\tanh s)}}{\sinh s}ds.

Defining the holomorphic function
G
by

(9.6.25)

G(z)=\frac{\exp{2i(\omega z-\kappa\tanh z)}}{2\pi\sinh z},

we see that
G
has simple poles at
i\pi\mathbb{Z}
and essential singularities at

i\pi(\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z})
.
For
R\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\backslash\frac{\pi}{2}\mathbb{Z},\epsilon\in(0,\pi/2)
, we have

(9.6.26)

\oint_{[-R,-\epsilon]\cup[\epsilon,R]}G(z)dz+\oint_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{\epsilon}^{-}\\
\gamma_{\epsilon}^{-}(\theta)=\epsilon e^{i\theta}\\
-\pi\leq t\leq 0\end{subarray}}G(z)dz+\oint_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{R}^{+}\\
\gamma_{R}^{+}(\theta)=Re^{i\theta}\\
0\leq t\leq\pi\end{subarray}}G(z)dz\\
=2i\pi\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
k\pi<2R\end{subarray}}\text{\tt Res}(G,ik\pi/2).

Claim 9.37
.

We have

\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\oint_{\gamma_{\epsilon}^{-}}G(z)dz=\frac{i}{2}
.

Proof.

Indeed we have

\int_{-\pi}^{0}\frac{\exp{2i(\omega\epsilon e^{i\theta}-\kappa\tanh(\epsilon e^{i\theta}))}}{2\pi\sinh(\epsilon e^{i\theta})}i\epsilon e^{i\theta}d\theta\\
=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{0}\frac{e^{2i\omega\epsilon e^{i\theta}}\epsilon e^{i\theta}}{\sinh(\epsilon e^{i\theta})}\exp{(-2i\kappa\tanh(\epsilon e^{i\theta}))}d\theta,

and since the function

z\mapsto\frac{ze^{2i\omega z}}{\sinh z}e^{-2i\kappa\tanh z}
is holomorphic near 0 with value 1 at 0, we get the result of the claim.
∎

Lemma 9.38
.

We have

\lim_{\mathbb{N}\ni m\rightarrow+{\infty}}\operatorname{Im}\left(\oint_{\gamma_{\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}}^{+}}G(z)dz\right)=0.

Proof.

Indeed we have with
R=\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}
,

\operatorname{Im}\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{\exp{2i(\omega Re^{i\theta}-\kappa\tanh(Re^{i\theta}))}}{2\pi\sinh(Re^{i\theta})}iRe^{i\theta}d\theta\\
=\frac{R}{\pi}\operatorname{Re}\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{e^{2i\omega R\cos{\theta}}e^{-2R\omega\sin\theta}e^{i\theta}}{1-e^{-2Re^{i\theta}}}e^{-Re^{i\theta}}\exp{(-2i\kappa\tanh(Re^{i\theta}))}d\theta\\
=\frac{2R}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\frac{e^{2i\omega R\cos{\theta}}e^{-2R\omega\sin\theta}e^{i\theta}}{1-e^{-2Re^{i\theta}}}e^{-Re^{i\theta}}\exp{(-2i\kappa\tanh(Re^{i\theta}))}\right\}d\theta,

so that

(9.6.27)

\operatorname{Im}\Bigl(\oint_{\gamma_{\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}}^{+}}G(z)dz\Bigr)\\
=\frac{2R}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}e^{-R\cos\theta}e^{-2R\omega\sin\theta}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\frac{e^{2i\omega R\cos{\theta}}e^{i\theta}}{1-e^{-2Re^{i\theta}}}e^{-iR\sin\theta}\exp{(-2i\kappa\tanh(Re^{i\theta}))}\right\}d\theta.

We have also

(9.6.28)

\displaystyle\tanh(Re^{i\theta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-2Re^{i\theta}}}{1+e^{-2Re^{i\theta}}}.

Claim 9.39
.

Defining for
m\in\mathbb{N},\theta\in[0,\pi]
,

g_{m}(\theta)=1-e^{-(\frac{\pi}{2}+m\pi)e^{i\theta}},

we find that

(9.6.29)

\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in[0,\pi]\\
m\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}|g_{m}(\theta)|=\beta_{0}>0,\quad\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in[0,\pi]\\
m\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}|2-g_{m}(\theta)|=\beta_{1}>0.

Proof of the claim.

If it were not the case, we could find sequences
\theta_{l}\in[0,\pi],m_{l}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(9.6.30)

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}e^{-(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)e^{i\theta_{l}}}=1.

Taking the logarithm of the modulus of both sides, we would get

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)\cos\theta_{l}=0,

i.e.

\cos\theta_{l}=\frac{\epsilon_{l}}{\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi}
,

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}\epsilon_{l}=0
.
Going back to (
9.6.30
), we find then

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}e^{-i(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)\sin\theta_{l}}=1,

i.e. since
\sin\theta_{l}\geq 0
,

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}\exp-i\Bigl\{(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)\Bigl(1-\frac{\epsilon_{l}^{2}}{(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)^{2}}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr\}=1,

implying

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}e^{-i(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)}=1,

which is not possible since

e^{-i(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)}=-i(-1)^{m_{l}}\in\{\pm i\},

proving the first inequality of the claim.
The second inequality follows from the same
reductio ad absurdum
,
starting with

(9.6.31)

\lim_{l\rightarrow+{\infty}}e^{-(\frac{\pi}{2}+m_{l}\pi)e^{i\theta_{l}}}=-1,

ending-up with an impossibility since
-1\notin\{\pm i\}
.
∎

As a consequence of Claim
9.39
and (
9.6.28
), we obtain
for
R=\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}
,
\theta\in(0,\pi)
,

(9.6.32)

|\tanh(Re^{i\theta})|\leq\frac{2}{\beta_{1}}.

Formula (
9.6.27
) gives then

(9.6.33)

\left|\operatorname{Im}\left(\oint_{\gamma_{\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}}^{+}}G(z)dz\right)\right|\leq\frac{2R}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}e^{-R\cos\theta}e^{-2R\omega\sin\theta}\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}\exp{(4\kappa/\beta_{1})}d\theta,

where, for
\omega>0
, the right-hand-side goes to zero when
R
goes to
+{\infty}
,
completing the proof of Lemma
9.38
.
∎

Lemma 9.40
.

With
G
defined in (
9.6.25
),
we have

(9.6.34)

2\pi\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\text{\tt Res}\left(G,ik\pi/2\right)=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{i(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right).

Proof.

We have

\text{\tt Res}(G,ik\pi/2)=\text{\tt Res}(G_{k},0)

and
with
k=2l
,

G_{k}(z)=\frac{\exp{2i(\omega(z+\frac{ik\pi}{2})-\kappa\tanh(z+\frac{ik\pi}{2}))}}{2\pi\sinh(z+\frac{ik\pi}{2})}=\frac{e^{-2l\pi\omega}e^{2i\omega z}e^{-2i\kappa\tanh z}}{2\pi(-1)^{l}\sinh z},

so that

(9.6.35)

\text{\tt Res}(G_{2l},0)=\frac{(-1)^{l}e^{-2l\pi\omega}}{2\pi},

whereas for
k=2l+1
, we have

G_{2l+1}(z)=\frac{\exp{2i(\omega(z+il\pi+\frac{i\pi}{2})-\kappa\tanh(z+il\pi+\frac{i\pi}{2}))}}{2\pi\sinh(z+il\pi+\frac{i\pi}{2})}\\
=\frac{e^{-(2l+1)\pi\omega}e^{2i\omega z}e^{-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{2\pi(-1)^{l}i\cosh z},

so that

(9.6.36)

\text{\tt Res}(G_{2l+1},0)=\frac{(-1)^{l}e^{-(2l+1)\pi\omega}}{2\pi i}\text{\tt Res}\Bigl(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\Bigr),

yielding

2\pi\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\text{\tt Res}(G,ik\pi/2)\\
=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{N}}{(-1)^{l}e^{-2l\pi\omega}}+\sum_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{(-1)^{l}e^{-(2l+1)\pi\omega}}{i}\text{\tt Res}\Bigl(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\Bigr),\\
=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{i(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\text{\tt Res}\Bigl(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\Big),

concluding the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proposition 9.41
.

Using the notations (
9.6.2
), with
a_{11}
defined in (
9.6.23
)
(see also (
9.6.24
)), we have for
\tau>0,\sigma\geq 0
,

(9.6.37)

a_{11}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}\operatorname{Im}\left\{\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i(\omega z-\kappa\operatorname{coth}z)}}{\cosh z},0\right)\right\}.

Proof.

Taking the imaginary part of both sides in (
9.6.26
), and letting

R\rightarrow+{\infty}
,

\epsilon\rightarrow 0_{+}
, we get, using (
9.6.34
),
(
9.6.24
), Claim
9.37
,

a_{11}-\frac{1}{2}+\operatorname{Im}\frac{i}{2}=\operatorname{Im}i\Bigl(\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{i(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\text{\tt Res}\bigl(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\bigr)\Bigr),

which is (
9.6.37
).
∎

Remark 9.42
.

In particular, when
\sigma=0
, we find for
\tau>0

(9.6.38)

1-a_{11}(\tau,0)=\frac{e^{-4\pi^{2}\tau}}{1+e^{-4\pi^{2}\tau}},

and since (
5.2.33
) implies that

\displaystyle 2\pi\operatorname{Re}a_{12}(\tau,0)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\sin(4\pi t\tau)}{\cosh t}dt=\operatorname{Im}\langle e^{i4\pi\tau t}H(t),\operatorname{sech}t\rangle_{\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{t}),\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}_{t})}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Im}\frac{1}{4i\pi\tau}\langle\frac{d}{dt}\bigl\{e^{i4\pi\tau t}\bigr\}H(t),\operatorname{sech}t\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Im}\frac{1}{4i\pi\tau}\left(\langle\frac{d}{dt}\bigl\{e^{i4\pi\tau t}H(t)\bigr\},\operatorname{sech}t\rangle-\langle\delta_{0},\operatorname{sech}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\tau}-\operatorname{Im}\frac{1}{4i\pi\tau}\langle e^{i4\pi\tau t}H(t),\operatorname{sech}^{\prime}(t)\rangle=\frac{1}{4\pi\tau}+O(\tau^{-3}),\quad\tau\rightarrow+{\infty},

we readily find that

\operatorname{Re}a_{12}(\tau,0)\gg 1-a_{11}(\tau,0),\quad\tau\rightarrow+{\infty},

providing another proof of Theorem
5.20
in the case
\sigma=0
.

Remark 9.43
.

The equation (
5.2.53
) gives also

\operatorname{Im}a_{12}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{e^{-2\pi^{2}\tau}}{2}a_{11}(\tau,\sigma),

where (
5.2.31
) gives, using the notations (
9.6.2
),

(9.6.39)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}a_{12}(\tau,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\cos(t\omega-{2\kappa}{\operatorname{coth}(t/2)})}{\cosh({t}/2)}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\cos\bigl(2(t\omega-{\kappa}{\operatorname{coth}t})\bigr)}{\cosh{t}}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\cos\bigl(2(t\omega-{\kappa}{\operatorname{coth}t})\bigr)}{\cosh{t}}dt.

With
G
given by (
9.6.25
), we note that

(9.6.40)

\tilde{G}(z)=\frac{ie^{\pi\omega}}{2}G(z+\frac{i\pi}{2})=\frac{\exp{2i(\omega z-\kappa\operatorname{coth}z)}}{4\pi\cosh z},

an holomorphic function with simple poles at
{i\pi}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z})

and essential singularities at
{i\pi}\mathbb{Z}
.
Following now for
\tilde{G}

the track of
G
in Claim
9.37
,
Lemmas
9.38
,
9.40
and Proposition
9.41
,
we get

(9.6.41)

\operatorname{Im}a_{12}(\tau,\sigma)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\rightarrow+{\infty}\\
\epsilon\rightarrow 0_{+}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Re}\oint_{[-R_{m},-\epsilon]\cup[\epsilon,R_{m}]}\tilde{G}(z)dz,\quad R_{m}=\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2},

and we have also

(9.6.42)

\oint_{[-R_{m},-\epsilon]\cup[\epsilon,R_{m}]}\tilde{G}(z)dz-\oint_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{\epsilon}^{+}\\
\gamma_{\epsilon}^{+}(\theta)=\epsilon e^{i\theta}\\
0\leq t\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\tilde{G}(z)dz+\oint_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{R_{m}}^{+}\\
\gamma_{R_{m}}^{+}(\theta)=R_{m}e^{i\theta}\\
0\leq t\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\tilde{G}(z)dz\\
=2i\pi\sum_{1\leq k\leq m}\text{\tt Res}(\tilde{G},ik\pi/2)=-\pi{e^{\pi\omega}}\sum_{1\leq k\leq m}\text{\tt Res}\left(G(\zeta+\frac{ik\pi}{2}+\frac{i\pi}{2}),0\right)\\
=-\pi{e^{\pi\omega}}\sum_{2\leq l\leq m+1}\text{\tt Res}\left(G(\zeta+\frac{il\pi}{2}),0\right).

Claim 9.44
.

We have

\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\oint_{\gamma_{\epsilon}^{+}}\tilde{G}(z)dz=0.

Proof.

Indeed, we have

-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}\epsilon e^{i\theta}={-2i\kappa\frac{1+e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}}{1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}}}

and for
\theta\in(0,\pi)
,

\operatorname{Im}\bigl(\frac{1+e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}}{1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}}\bigr)=\operatorname{Im}\frac{(1+e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}})(1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{-i\theta}})}{|1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|^{2}}=\operatorname{Im}\frac{e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}-e^{-2\epsilon e^{-i\theta}}}{|1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|^{2}}\\
=e^{-2\epsilon\cos\theta}\operatorname{Im}\frac{e^{-2\epsilon i\sin\theta}-e^{2\epsilon i\sin\theta}}{|1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|^{2}}=e^{-2\epsilon\cos\theta}\operatorname{Im}\frac{-2i\sin(2\epsilon\sin\theta)}{|1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|^{2}}\\
=-2e^{-2\epsilon\cos\theta}\frac{\sin(2\epsilon\sin\theta)}{|1-e^{-2\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|^{2}}\leq 0,\quad\text{if $\epsilon\leq\pi/4$,}

so that

|e^{-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|\leq 1,

implying

4\pi\left|\oint_{\gamma_{\epsilon}^{+}}\tilde{G}(z)dz\right|\leq\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{|e^{i\omega\epsilon e^{i\theta}}|}{|\cosh\epsilon e^{i\theta}|}\epsilon|ie^{i\theta}|d\theta=\epsilon\int_{0}^{\pi}\frac{e^{-\omega\epsilon\sin\theta}}{|\cosh\epsilon e^{i\theta}|}d\theta,

which goes to zero when
\epsilon\rightarrow 0_{+}
, concluding the proof of Claim
9.44
.
∎

Claim 9.45
.

We have

\lim_{\mathbb{N}\ni m\rightarrow+{\infty}}\oint_{\gamma_{\frac{\pi}{4}+m\frac{\pi}{2}}^{+}}\tilde{G}(z)dz=0.

Proof.

Indeed, we have, using Claim
9.39
,

|\operatorname{coth}(R_{m}e^{i\theta})|=\Bigl|\frac{1+e^{-2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}{1-e^{-2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}\Bigr|\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1+e^{-2R_{m}\cos\theta}}{\beta_{0}}\leq\frac{2}{\beta_{0},}&\text{for $\theta\in[0,\pi/2],$}\\
\\
\left|\frac{1+e^{2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}{1-e^{2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}\right|\leq\frac{2}{\beta_{0}},&\text{for $\theta\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\pi],$}\end{cases}

so that

|\tilde{G}(R_{m}e^{i\theta})iR_{m}e^{i\theta}|\leq R_{m}e^{4\kappa/\beta_{0}}e^{-2\omega R_{m}\sin\theta}\begin{cases}\left|\frac{2e^{-R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}{1+e^{-2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}\right|\leq\frac{2e^{-R_{m}\cos\theta}}{\beta_{1}}&\text{for $\theta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]$,}\\
\left|\frac{2e^{R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}{1+e^{2R_{m}e^{i\theta}}}\right|\leq\frac{2e^{R_{m}\cos\theta}}{\beta_{1}}&\text{for $\theta\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\pi]$,}\end{cases}\\
\leq\frac{2R_{m}}{\beta_{1}}e^{4\kappa/\beta_{0}}e^{-2\omega R_{m}\sin\theta-R_{m}|\cos\theta|},

which goes to 0 when
m
goes to
+{\infty}
, proving the claim.
∎

Using (
9.6.34
),
we calculate now

\displaystyle 2\pi\sum_{l\geq 2}

\displaystyle\text{\tt Res}\left(G(\zeta+\frac{il\pi}{2}),0\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{i(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.0pt-2\pi\Bigl(\text{\tt Res}\left(G,i\pi/2\right)+\text{\tt Res}\left(G,0\right)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{i(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.0pt+ie^{-\pi\omega}\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)-1

\displaystyle=-\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}-i\left(\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}-e^{-\pi\omega}\right)\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+ie^{-\pi\omega}\left(\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}\right)\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right),

so that
from (
9.6.41
), (
9.6.42
), Claims
9.44
&
9.45
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}a_{12}(\tau,\sigma)

\displaystyle=-\pi e^{\pi\omega}\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(-\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}-e^{-\pi\omega}\left(\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}\right)\operatorname{Im}\Bigl\{\text{\tt Res}\Bigl({\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{\pi\omega}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}+e^{-\pi\omega}\left(\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{1+e^{-2\pi\omega}}\right)\operatorname{Im}\Bigl\{\text{\tt Res}\Bigl({\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\right),

so that

(9.6.43)

\operatorname{Im}a_{12}(\tau,\sigma)\\
=\frac{e^{-\pi\omega}}{2(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}+\frac{e^{-2\pi\omega}}{2(1+e^{-2\pi\omega})}\operatorname{Im}\left\{\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)\right\},

recovering (
9.6.37
) from (
5.2.53
).

N.B
.

We note that

(9.6.44)

\displaystyle\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{2i\omega z-2i\kappa\operatorname{coth}z}}{\cosh z},0\right)=\frac{1}{2}\text{\tt Res}\left(\frac{e^{i(\omega z-2\kappa\operatorname{coth}(z/2))}}{\cosh(z/2)},0\right),

so that (
9.6.43
) corroborates
(\text{A}14)
in
[
55
]
;
however, we were not able to understand formulas

(\text{A}10)
,
(\text{A}11)
and
(20)
in
[
55
]
.

9.7.
Airy function

9.7.1.
Standard results on the Airy function

We collect in this section a couple of classical results on the Airy function
(see e.g. Definition 7.6.8 in Section 7.6 of
[
23
]
or the references
[
51
]
,

[
49
]
,
[
29
]
). For all the statements of this section whose proofs are not included, we refer the reader to Chapter 9 of
[
35
]
.

Definition 9.46
.

The Airy function
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}
is defined as the inverse Fourier transform of
\xi\mapsto e^{i(2\pi\xi)^{3}/3}
.

Proposition 9.47
.

For any
h>0
and all
x\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

(9.7.1)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int e^{\frac{i}{3}(\xi+ih)^{3}}e^{ix(\xi+ih)}d\xi=e^{-xh}e^{\frac{h^{3}}{3}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int e^{-h\xi^{2}}e^{i(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}e^{ix\xi}d\xi.

We note that the function
\mathbb{R}\ni\xi\mapsto e^{\frac{i}{3}(\xi+ih)^{3}}
belongs to the Schwartz space
for any
h>0
since

\frac{i}{3}(\xi+ih)^{3}=-h\xi^{2}+\frac{h^{3}}{3}+i\bigl(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2}\bigr),

so that

e^{\frac{i}{3}(\xi+ih)^{3}}=e^{-h\xi^{2}}e^{i(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}e^{h^{3}/3}.

Theorem 9.48
.

The Airy function
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}
is an entire function on
\mathbb{C}
, real-valued on the real line, which is the unique solution of the initial value problem for the Airy equation

(9.7.2)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime\prime}(x)-x\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=0,\quad\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(0)=\frac{3^{-1/6}\Gamma(1/3)}{2\pi},\quad\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(0)=-\frac{3^{1/6}\Gamma(2/3)}{2\pi}.

We have also, for any
x\in\mathbb{C}
,

(9.7.3)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}e^{-\xi^{3}/3}e^{-x\xi/2}\cos\bigl(\frac{x\xi\sqrt{3}}{2}+\frac{\pi}{6}\bigr)d\xi,

and the power series expansion of the Airy function is

(9.7.4)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=\frac{1}{\pi 3^{2/3}}\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{(3^{1/3}x)^{k}}{k!}\Gamma\bigl(\frac{k+1}{3}\bigr)\sin\bigl(2(k+1)\frac{\pi}{3}\bigr).

Lemma 9.49
.

For
x\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{R}_{-}
, we have

(9.7.5)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{2}{3}x^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-x^{1/2}\xi^{2}}e^{i\xi^{3}/3}d\xi.

Proof.

Using Proposition
9.47
, we get (
9.7.5
) for
x>0
(choosing
h=x^{1/2}
), and then we may use
an analytic continuation argument.
∎

Theorem 9.50
.

For all
M\in\mathbb{N}
, for all

x\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{R}_{-}
, we have

(9.7.6)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{2x^{3/2}}{3}}x^{-1/4}\left\{\sum_{0\leq l\leq M}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{3^{2l}(2l)!}\Gamma\bigl(3l+\frac{1}{2}\bigr)x^{-3l/2}+R_{M}(x)\right\},\\
\text{with}\quad|R_{M}(x)|\leq\frac{\Gamma\bigl(3M+3+\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}{3^{2M+2}(2M+2)!}|x|^{-\frac{3(M+1)}{2})}\left(\cos(\frac{\arg x}{2})\right)^{-3(M+1)-\frac{1}{2}}.

For
x<0
, we have

(9.7.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|x|^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigl(\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|x|^{3/2}\bigr)+O(|x|^{-3/2})\Bigr),

(9.7.8)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{|x|^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|x|^{3/2}\bigr)+O(|x|^{-3/2})\Bigr).

Lemma 9.51
.

With
j=e^{2i\pi/3}
we have for all
x\in\mathbb{C}
,

(9.7.9)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)+j\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(jx)+j^{2}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(j^{2}x)=0.

In particular for
r\geq 0
, we have

(9.7.10)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-r)=2\operatorname{Re}\bigl(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(re^{\frac{i\pi}{3}})\bigr).

Lemma 9.52
.

The zeroes of the Airy function are simple and located on
(-{\infty},0)
. We shall use the notation

(9.7.11)

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{-1}(\{0\})=\{\eta_{k}\}_{k\geq 0},\quad\eta_{k+1}<\eta_{k}<0,\quad\lim_{k\rightarrow+{\infty}}\eta_{k}=-{\infty}.

The largest zero of
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}
is
\eta_{0}\approx-2.338107410
and
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta)
is positive for
\eta>\eta_{0}
. We have also for all
k\geq 0
,

(9.7.12)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta_{2k+1})=0,\ \operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(\eta_{2k+1})<0,\enskip\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta_{2k})=0,\ \operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(\eta_{2k})>0,

(9.7.13)

\displaystyle\text{$\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta)<0$ \ for $\eta\in(\eta_{2k+1},\eta_{2k})$},\ \operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta)>0\text{\ for $\eta\in(\eta_{2k+2},\eta_{2k+1}),$}

(9.7.14)

\displaystyle\text{$\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)>0$ for $\eta\in(\eta_{2k+1},\eta_{2k})$},\ \text{$\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)<0$ for $\eta\in(\eta_{2k+2},\eta_{2k+1})$}.

N.B
.

The simplicity of the zeroes of the Airy function holds true for any non-zero solution of the Airy differential equation

y^{\prime\prime}=xy.

The solutions of this ODE are analytic functions and if
a
is a double zero, we have

y(a)=y^{\prime}(a)=0
and thus from the Airy equation, we get
y^{\prime\prime}(a)=0
; we may then prove by induction on
k\geq 1
that
y^{(l)}(a)=0
for
0\leq l\leq k+1
: it is proven for
k=1
, and if true for some
k\geq 1
, we get

y^{(k+2)}(x)=\bigl(xy(x)\bigr)^{(k)}\Longrightarrow y^{(k+2)}(a)=0,

proving the final step in the induction; as a consequence, the function has a zero of infinite order, which is impossible for a non-zero analytic function.
Assertion (
9.7.14
) follows from the Airy differential equation
(
9.7.2
), from (
9.7.13
) and
\eta_{2k}<0
.

Remark 9.53
.

For
M=0
,
|\arg x|\leq\pi/3
, we have

|R_{0}(x)|\leq\frac{\Gamma\bigl(3+\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}{3^{2}(2)!}|x|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\Bigl(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\Bigr)^{-\frac{7}{2}}=|x|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{5}{3^{11/4}\sqrt{2}}\leq|x|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\times 0.305455,

so that

(9.7.15)

\displaystyle|R_{0}(x)|\leq 0.305455|x|^{-3/2}\quad\text{if $|\arg x|\leq\pi/3$,}

(9.7.16)

\displaystyle\text{and for $|x|\geq 12$, $|\arg x|\leq\pi/3$ we have}\quad|R_{0}(x)|\leq 0.007349.

We get then for
\lambda>0
, using (
9.7.10
)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Re}\Bigl(e^{i\pi/3}\lambda^{-1/4}e^{-i\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}\bigl(\sqrt{\pi}e^{-i\pi/12}+R_{0}(\lambda e^{i\pi/3})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle{\hskip 15.0pt}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\lambda^{-1/4}\cos(\frac{\pi}{4}-\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Re}\Bigl\{\lambda^{-1/4}R_{0}(re^{i\pi/3})e^{i\pi/4}e^{-i\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle{\hskip 15.0pt}{\hskip 15.0pt}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\lambda^{-1/4}\Bigl(\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\operatorname{Re}\bigl\{R_{0}(\lambda e^{i\pi/3})e^{i\pi/4}e^{-i\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}\bigr\}\Bigr),

so that

(9.7.17)

for
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-\lambda)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\lambda^{-1/4}\Bigl(\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+\tilde{R}_{0}(\lambda)\Bigr),

(9.7.18)

with

\displaystyle|\tilde{R}_{0}(\lambda)|\leq\lambda^{-3/2}\times 0.172335,

(9.7.19)

and for
\lambda\geq 12
,

\displaystyle|\tilde{R}_{0}(\lambda)|\leq 0.004146.

Remark 9.54
.

For
M=1
,
|\arg x|\leq\pi/3
, we have

(9.7.20)

|R_{1}(x)|\leq\frac{\Gamma\bigl(6+\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}{3^{4}(4)!}|x|^{-3}\Bigl(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\Bigr)^{-6-\frac{1}{2}}\\
=|x|^{-3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{11!}{2^{21/2}\times 3^{37/4}\times 5}\leq|x|^{-3}\times 0.377203,

and

(9.7.21)

\text{for $|x|\geq 12$,\quad}|R_{1}(x)|\leq 0.000219,

so that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}r^{-1/4}\Bigl(\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2})+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}\sin(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4})r^{-3/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 184.0pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\operatorname{Re}\bigl\{R_{1}(re^{i\pi/3})e^{i\pi/4}e^{-i\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}}\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}r^{-1/4}\Bigl(\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2})+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}\sin(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4})r^{-3/2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\tilde{R}_{1}(r)\Bigr),

with

(9.7.22)

\displaystyle\text{for $r>0$,\quad}|\tilde{R}_{1}(r)|

\displaystyle\leq r^{-3}\times 0.377203,

(9.7.23)

\displaystyle\text{for $r\geq 12$,\quad}|\tilde{R}_{1}(r)|

\displaystyle\leq 0.000219.

We find for
\lambda>0
,

(9.7.24)

G(-\lambda)\\
=\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigl(\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}r^{-3/2}\sin\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\tilde{R}_{1}(r)\Bigr)dr,

and we have

\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{3/4}\sqrt{\pi}}r^{1/2}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr\\
={\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\frac{1}{\lambda^{3/4}\sqrt{\pi}}}-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{7/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr,

as well as

-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{7/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr=-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{9/4}\sqrt{\pi}}r^{1/2}\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr\\
{=\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\lambda^{-9/4}-\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{9}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-13/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr,}

so that

(9.7.25)

\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr={\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\frac{1}{\lambda^{3/4}\sqrt{\pi}}}\\
+\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\lambda^{-9/4}-\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{9}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-13/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr.

We have also

(9.7.26)

\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{r^{1/4}}\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}r^{-3/2}\sin\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)dr=\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-7/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)dr\\
=-\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)\lambda^{-9/4}+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}\frac{9}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)r^{-13/4}dr,

so that
(
9.7.25
),
(
9.7.26
)
and (
9.7.24
) entail

\displaystyle G(-\lambda)

\displaystyle={\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\frac{1}{\lambda^{3/4}\sqrt{\pi}}}+\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\lambda^{-9/4}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3}{4\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{9}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-13/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)\lambda^{-9/4}+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\pi}\frac{9}{4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)r^{-13/4}dr

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-1/4}\tilde{R}_{1}(r).

We get then

\displaystyle G(-\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{-3/4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Biggl(\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)+\frac{3}{4}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\lambda^{-6/4}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt-\frac{3}{4}\times\frac{9}{4}\lambda^{3/4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-13/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)dr

\displaystyle\hskip 80.0pt-\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)\lambda^{-6/4}

\displaystyle\hskip 90.0pt+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{9}{4}\lambda^{3/4}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\cos\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)r^{-13/4}dr

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+\frac{\lambda^{3/4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}r^{-1/4}\tilde{R}_{1}(r)\Biggr),

so that

(9.7.27)

G(-\lambda)=\frac{\lambda^{-3/4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)+\lambda^{-3/2}S_{1}(\lambda)\Bigr),

with

(9.7.28)

|S_{1}(\lambda)|\leq\frac{3}{4}+\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{18\sqrt{\pi}}+\frac{4}{9\sqrt{\pi}}\times 0.377203\leq 1.80293

where we have used (
9.7.22
) for the bound of the last term above.
As a consequence, if
\lambda\geq 12
, we get that

(9.7.29)

|\lambda^{-3/2}S_{1}(\lambda)|\leq 0.0433716.

This is allowing us to extend the proof of Lemma
9.60
to all values.
Note that the first 10 values (and more) are accessible numerically.

Since we have
\eta_{9}=-12.82877675<-12
,
Formulas (

), (

), (
9.7.27
), (

) imply the following result.

.

With
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}
and

(9.7.30)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\lambda^{-1/4}\Bigl(\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+\tilde{R}_{0}(\lambda)\Bigr),

9.7.2.
More on the Airy function

Proposition 9.56
.

We have

(9.7.34)

\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx=\frac{1}{3}.

Proof.

According to Theorem
9.50
, the Airy function
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}
is rapidily decreasing on the positive half-line
and thus belongs to
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, so that the integral in (
9.7.34
) makes sense.
Also we have from Theorem
9.50
and the Lebesgue Dominated Convergence Theorem that,

(9.7.35)

\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0_{+}}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dxe^{-h^{3}/3},

and we shall now calculate the right-hand-side of (
9.7.35
).
We have for
h>0
,

\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dxe^{-h^{3}/3}=\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int e^{-h\xi^{2}}e^{i(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}e^{ix\xi}d\xi dx=\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\widehat{\psi_{h}}(-x)dx,

with

(9.7.36)

\psi_{h}(\xi)=e^{-h(2\pi\xi)^{2}}e^{i(\frac{(2\pi\xi)^{3}}{3}-(2\pi\xi)h^{2})},

so that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dxe^{-h^{3}/3}

\displaystyle=\langle\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\text{pv}\frac{1}{\xi},\psi_{h}\rangle_{\mathscr{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{S}}

\displaystyle{\hskip 15.0pt}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\langle\text{pv}\frac{1}{\xi},{e^{-h(2\pi\xi)^{2}}e^{i(\frac{(2\pi\xi)^{3}}{3}-(2\pi\xi)h^{2})}}\rangle

\displaystyle{\hskip 15.0pt}{\hskip 15.0pt}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\langle\text{pv}\frac{1}{\xi},{e^{-h\xi^{2}}\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}\rangle.

We note at this point that, according to (
4.2.9
), the right-hand-side of the above equality
is for
h=0
equal to

\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{\pi}{3}=\frac{1}{3},

so that, with (
9.7.35
), we are left to proving that

(9.7.37)

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0_{+}}\langle\text{pv}\frac{1}{\xi},{e^{-h\xi^{2}}\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}\rangle=\frac{\pi}{3}.

We have

\int\frac{\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})}{\xi}e^{-h\xi^{2}}d\xi=\frac{\pi}{3}+\int\frac{\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}-\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})}{\xi}d\xi\\
=\frac{\pi}{3}+\underbrace{\int\frac{\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})}{\xi}\bigl(\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-1\bigr)d\xi}_{I_{1}(h)}-\underbrace{\int\frac{\sin(\xi h^{2})}{\xi}\cos(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})e^{-h\xi^{2}}d\xi}_{I_{2}(h)}.

We have

I_{1,1}(h)=\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\xi^{2}\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})}{\xi^{3}}\bigl(\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-1\bigr)d\xi\\
=\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\frac{d}{d\xi}(\cos(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3}))}{\xi^{3}}\bigl(\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-1\bigr)d\xi,

and a simple integration by parts
38
38

38

The boundary term is easy to handle and for the derivative falling on
\xi^{-3}
, we use that

|\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-1|\leq 2
; if the derivative falls on the other term we get

\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}\frac{\cos(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})}{\xi^{3}}\bigl(2h\xi\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}+e^{-h\xi^{2}}\sin(\xi h^{2})h^{2}\bigr)d\xi,

which goes trivially to 0 with
h
.
shows that

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}I_{1,1}(h)=0
; we have also trivially that

0=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\xi^{2}\sin(\frac{\xi^{3}}{3})}{\xi^{3}}\bigl(\cos(\xi h^{2})e^{-h\xi^{2}}-1\bigr)d\xi.

On the other hand, we have

|I_{2}(h)|\leq\int h^{2}e^{-h\xi^{2}}d\xi=O(h^{3/2}),

which completes the proof of (
9.7.37
) as well as the proof of Proposition
9.56
.
∎

Lemma 9.57
.

We have

(9.7.38)

\lim_{R\rightarrow+{\infty}}\int_{-R}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx=\frac{2}{3}.

Proof.

Using (
9.7.7
), we find for
R\geq 1
,

\int_{-R}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx=\int_{0}^{R}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-r)dr=\int_{0}^{1}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-r)dr\\
+\int_{1}^{R}\Bigl(\frac{1}{r^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)+O(r^{-7/4})\Bigr)dr,

proving that the limit in the left-hand-side of (
9.7.38
) is existing.

Claim 9.58
.

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0_{+}}\int_{-{\infty}}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dx=\int_{-{\infty}}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx.

Proof of the Claim.

We have

\int_{-{\infty}}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dx=\int_{-{\infty}}^{-1}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dx+\underbrace{\int_{-1}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dx}_{\text{with limit $\int_{-1}^{0}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx$}}

and using (
9.7.7
), we have only to check

\int_{-{\infty}}^{-1}|x|^{-1/4}e^{xh+i\frac{2}{3}|x|^{3/2}}dx=\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}t^{-1/4}e^{-th+i\frac{2}{3}t^{3/2}}dt\\
=-\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}\frac{d}{dt}\left\{e^{-th+i\frac{2}{3}t^{3/2}}\right\}(h-it^{1/2})^{-1}t^{-1/4}dt=e^{-h+i\frac{2}{3}}(h-i)^{-1}\\
+\int_{1}^{+{\infty}}e^{-th+i\frac{2}{3}t^{3/2}}\bigl((h-it^{1/2})^{-2}\frac{i}{2}t^{-3/4}-(h-it^{1/2})^{-1}\frac{1}{4}t^{-5/4}\bigr)dt,

and since the absolute value of the integrand in the last integral is bounded above by

\frac{3}{4}t^{-7/4}
, we get the result of the Claim.
∎

With (
9.7.35
), (
9.7.36
),
this gives

\int_{-{\infty}}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)dx=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0_{+}}\int_{-{\infty}}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(x)e^{xh}dxe^{-h^{3}/3}=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0_{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\psi_{h}}(-\xi)d\xi=\psi_{h}(0)\right)=1,

and Proposition
9.56
provides the result of the lemma.
∎

9.7.3.
Asymptotic expansion for the function
G
defined in (
4.2.8
)

Lemma 9.59
.

With
G
defined in (
4.2.8
), we have

(9.7.39)

G(-\lambda)=\lambda^{-3/4}\pi^{-1/2}\sin(\frac{3\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+O(\lambda^{-9/4}),\quad\lambda\rightarrow+{\infty}.

Proof.

Property (
9.7.38
) and (
9.7.7
) give for
\eta=-\lambda<0
,

\displaystyle G(\eta)=\frac{2}{3}+\int_{0}^{\eta}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\xi)d\xi=\int_{-{\infty}}^{\eta}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\xi)d\xi=\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-r)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}2\operatorname{Re}\bigl(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(e^{\frac{i\pi}{3}}r)\bigr)dr\ \text{\tiny(we have used \eqref{929l}); we use now \eqref{995} for $M=1,x\in e^{i\pi/3}\mathbb{R}_{+}$)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda}^{+{\infty}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{r^{1/4}\sqrt{\pi}}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}\bigr)+\frac{\Gamma(7/2)}{3^{2}2\pi}r^{-7/4}\sin\bigl(\frac{2}{3}r^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)+O(r^{-13/4})\Bigr)dr

\displaystyle=(2/3)^{1/2}\pi^{-1/2}\int_{\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}s^{-1/2}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 75.0pt+\frac{(2/3)^{3/2}\Gamma(7/2)}{3^{2}2\pi}\int_{\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}s^{-3/2}\sin\bigl(s-\frac{\pi}{4}\bigr)ds+O(\lambda^{-9/4}).

We integrate by parts in the first integral with

\int_{\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}s^{-1/2}\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+s\bigr)ds=-\int_{\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}s^{-1/2}\frac{d}{ds}\left\{\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+s\bigr)\right\}ds

=(\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})^{-1/2}\cos(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+\int_{\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}(-1/2)s^{-3/2}\cos(\pi/4+s)ds.

We have to deal with two integrals of type

\int_{\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}s^{-3/2}\frac{d}{ids}e^{is}ds=i(\lambda^{3/2})^{(-3/2)}e^{i\lambda^{3/2}}-\frac{1}{i}\int_{\lambda^{3/2}}^{+{\infty}}(-3/2)s^{-5/2}e^{is}ds=O(\lambda^{-9/4}).

Eventually we find

G(-\lambda)=\lambda^{-3/4}\pi^{-1/2}\cos(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})+O(\lambda^{-9/4}).

∎

Lemma 9.60
.

The zeroes of the function
G
on the real line are simple and make a decreasing sequence of negative numbers
(\xi_{l})_{l\leq 0}
such that

(9.7.40)

\dots\eta_{2k+2}<\xi_{2k+2}<\eta_{2k+1}<\xi_{2k+1}<\eta_{2k}<\xi_{2k}\dots,\quad\xi_{0}\approx-1.38418.

The largest ten zeroes of
G
are given by the following table

\xi_{0}

\xi_{1}

\xi_{2}

\xi_{3}

-3.33004

-4.86074

-6.18885

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(9.7.41)

G(\eta_{2k})<0<G(\eta_{2k+1}),

and

G(\eta_{2k})
(resp.
G(\eta_{2k+1})
) is a local minimum (resp. maximum) of
G
near
\eta_{2k}
(resp.
\eta_{2k+1}
).
Moreover,
G(\eta_{0})
is an absolute minimum of the function
G
on the real line.

N.B
.

We claim also that

(9.7.42)

|G(\eta_{2k})|>G(\eta_{2k+1})>|G(\eta_{2k+2})|,

but shall not provide a complete proof for that statement, which is anyway not needed is our Section
4.3
.

Proof.

In the first place, we know that
G(\eta_{0})<0
and
G
strictly increases on
[\eta_{0},+{\infty})
so that
\xi_{0}\approx-1.38418
is defined as the unique zero of
G
on

(\eta_{0},0)
since
G(0)=2/3
. We may note that we found in particular that

(9.7.43)

\forall\eta>\eta_{0},\quad 1>G(\eta)>G(\eta_{0}).

Also, the first ten zeroes of
G
are simple and satisfy (
9.7.40
), (
9.7.41
) and (
9.7.42
).
Moreover, using Lemma
9.55
, we obtain that for
\lambda\geq 12
,

\displaystyle G(-\lambda)=0

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\bigl|\cos\bigl(\frac{3\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\bigr|\leq 0.0433716,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-\lambda)=0

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\bigr|\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)\bigr|\leq 0.004146,

As a result, if
-\lambda
is a double zero of
G
we must have
both inequalities above,
which is impossible.
As a result all zeroes of
G
are simple
39
39

39

It is not hard to obtain an asymptotic version of this, namely the same result for
\lambda

large enough. However, asymptotic methods provide asymptotic results and to get a result at a finite distance, we had
to use the numerical results of Lemma
9.55
, grounded on a numerical estimate of the constants appearing in Theorem
9.50
.

and located on
(-{\infty},0)
.
Let us consider the interval
[\eta_{2k+1},\eta_{2k}]
:
we have

\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta_{2k+1})=\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(\eta_{2k})=0,\quad\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(\eta_{2k+1})<0<\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(\eta_{2k}),\quad\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime\prime}>0\text{ on $(\eta_{2k+1},\eta_{2k})$.}

As a result, we obtain that
G
has a local minimum at
\eta_{2k}
and a local maximum at

\eta_{2k+1}
. Moreover we find from (
9.7.31
) in Lemma
9.55
and
k\geq 5
that

\max\Bigl(|\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k}|^{3/2})|,|\sin(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k+1}|^{3/2})|\Bigr)\leq 0.004146

which implies that

\min\Bigl(|\cos(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k}|^{3/2})|,|\cos(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k+1}|^{3/2})|\Bigr)\geq 0.99999.

We know that
\operatorname{\tt A\!i}^{\prime}(\eta_{2k})>0
,
which implies, thanks
40
40

40

Here this is proven if
k
is large enough from (
9.7.8
), and we leave to the reader the proof of a numerical estimate
analogous to Lemma
9.55
for the derivative of the Airy function.
A direct estimate is possible, using (
9.7.5
) and the identity (to be differentiated) for
\lambda>0
,

(9.7.44)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\tt A\!i}(-\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{-1/4}}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Bigl\{\sin\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2}\bigr)+a_{0}(\lambda)\lambda^{-3/2}\Bigr\},

(9.7.45)

\displaystyle a_{0}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{3/2}}{\pi}e^{i(\frac{\pi}{3}-\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{3/2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\xi^{2}\lambda^{1/2}e^{i\pi/6}}\bigl(\cos(\xi^{3}/3)-1\bigr)d\xi.

to (
9.7.8
)

\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k}|^{3/2}\bigr)\leq-0.99999,\qquad\cos\bigl(\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}|\eta_{2k+1}|^{3/2}\bigr)\geq 0.99999,

and Lemma
9.55
implies that

G(\eta_{2k})<0<G(\eta_{2k+1})
, which is (
9.7.41
).
Since the function
G
is strictly monotone decreasing
on the interval

[\eta_{2k+1},\eta_{2k}]
, it has a unique simple zero
\xi_{2k+1}

on the interior of this interval.
Analogously, we can prove that
on the interval

[\eta_{2k+2},\eta_{2k+1}]
, it has a unique simple zero
\xi_{2k+2}

on the interior of this interval,
proving that the sequence of zeroes of the function
G
is decreasing strictly with

\eta_{2k+2}<\xi_{2k+2}<\eta_{2k+1}<\xi_{2k+1}<\eta_{2k}<\xi_{2k},\quad k\geq 0.

We shall prove a weaker statement than (
9.7.42
): we know that

|G(\eta_{l})|<|G(\eta_{0})|)
for
1\leq l\leq 9
from the numerical values obtained above.
Moreover if
\lambda\geq 12
we find

|G(-\lambda)|\leq\lambda^{-3/4}\pi^{-1/2}(1+0.0433716)\leq 0.0913016<|G(\eta_{0})|=0.2743520591,

proving indeed that
G(\eta_{0})
is the absolute minimum of the function
G
on the real line,
since the desired estimate is proven for
\eta>\eta_{0}
and for
\eta<\eta_{0}
,
either
G(\eta)\geq 0
, or
-0.0913016\leq G(\eta)<0
if
\eta\leq-12
.
As said above, the values less than 12 are treated directly by a numerical calculation.
The proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

9.8.
Miscellaneous formulas

9.8.1.
Some elementary formulas

We define for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

(9.8.1)

\arctan\tau=\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{dt}{1+t^{2}},

and we note that
\arctan\tau\in(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
,

\forall\tau\in\mathbb{R},\ \tan(\arctan\tau)=\tau,\qquad\forall\theta\in(-\pi/2,\pi/2),\ \arctan(\tan\theta)=\theta.

Moreover we have for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

(9.8.2)

e^{i\arctan\tau}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\tau^{2}}}(1+i\tau),

since for
\theta\in(-\pi/2,\pi/2)
,
\tau=\tan\theta
, we have
1+\tau^{2}=\frac{1}{\cos^{2}\theta}
and thus

\cos\theta>0\Longrightarrow\cos\theta=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\tau^{2}}}\Longrightarrow-{\sin\theta}=-\frac{1}{2}(1+\tau^{2})^{-3/2}2\tau(1+\tau^{2}),

so that

e^{i\theta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\tau^{2}}}(1+i\tau).

Let
a\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
be given.
The Fourier transform of
\mathbf{1}_{[-a,a]}
is

(9.8.3)

\int_{-a}^{a}e^{-2i\pi x\xi}dx=2\int_{0}^{a}\cos(2\pi x\xi)dx=\frac{2}{2\pi\xi}[\sin(2\pi x\xi)]^{x=a}_{x=0}=\frac{\sin(2\pi a\xi)}{\pi\xi}.

9.8.2.
Taking the derivative of
F_{k}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}

We have, using a parity argument,

F_{k}(a)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sin a\tau}{\pi\tau}\frac{(1+i\tau)^{2k+1}}{(1+\tau^{2})^{k+1}}d\tau=\sum_{0\leq 2l\leq 2k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\sin a\tau}{\pi\tau}\frac{\binom{2k+1}{2l}(-1)^{l}\tau^{2l}}{(1+\tau^{2})^{k+1}}d\tau.

We see also that

1+2k+2-2l=2k+3-2l\geq 3

so that we can take the derivative of
F_{k}

and get

F^{\prime}_{k}(a)=\sum_{0\leq 2l\leq 2k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\cos a\tau}{\pi}\frac{\binom{2k+1}{2l}(-1)^{l}\tau^{2l}}{(1+\tau^{2})^{k+1}}d\tau=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}({\cos a\tau})\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{(1+i\tau)^{k}}{(1-i\tau)^{k+1}}\right)d\tau,

with absolutely converging integrals.
For
a>0
, we have

(9.8.4)

F^{\prime}_{k}(a)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}({\cos a\tau})\frac{(1+i\tau)^{k}}{(1-i\tau)^{k+1}}d\tau,

since

(9.8.5)

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow+{\infty}}\int_{-\lambda}^{\lambda}\frac{\tau^{j}\cos(a\tau)}{(1+\tau^{2})^{k+1}}d\tau\text{\quad makes sense for $j\leq 2k+1$ (and vanishes for $j$ odd).}

9.8.3.
A proof of the weak limit

We have for
u\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, according to (
1.2.3
),

\langle\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{2\pi(x^{2}+\xi^{2})\leq a\}\bigr)^{w}u,u\rangle=\iint_{2\pi(x^{2}+\xi^{2})\leq a}\mathcal{W}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi,

so that
implies

\sum_{k\geq 0}F_{k}(a)\langle\mathbb{P}_{k}u,u\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\iint_{2\pi(x^{2}+\xi^{2})\leq a}\mathcal{W}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi.

Choosing now
u=u_{k}
as a normalized eigenfunction of the Harmonic Oscillator
with eigenvalue
k+1/2
,
we obtain

F_{k}(a)=\iint_{2\pi(x^{2}+\xi^{2})\leq a}\mathcal{W}(u_{k},u_{k})(x,\xi)dxd\xi.

Since the function
(x,\xi)\mapsto\mathcal{W}(u_{k},u_{k})(x,\xi)
belongs to the Schwartz class of
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
we find that

\lim_{a\rightarrow+{\infty}}F_{k}(a)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\mathcal{H}(u_{k},u_{k})(x,\xi)dxd\xi=\|u_{k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=1,\quad\text{\tt qed.}

9.8.4.
A different normalization for the Wigner function

The paper
[
39
]
is using a different normalization for the Wigner distribution in
n
dimensions with

(9.8.6)

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}(u,v)(x,\xi)=(2\pi)^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}u(x+\frac{z}{2})\bar{v}(x-\frac{z}{2})e^{-iz\cdot\xi}dz.

The relationship with our definition (
1.1.6
) is

(9.8.7)

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}(u,v)(x,\xi)=\mathcal{W}(u,v)(x,\frac{\xi}{2\pi})(2\pi)^{-n}.

As a result, we find that

\mathcal{E}_{lo}\bigl(\mathbb{B}^{2n}(R)\bigr)=\sup_{\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=1}\iint_{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\leq R^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi,

is equal to

\sup_{\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=1}\iint_{|x|^{2}+4\pi^{2}|\xi|^{2}\leq R^{2}}\mathcal{W}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi\\
=\sup_{\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=1}\iint_{2\pi(|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2})\leq R^{2}}\mathcal{W}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi,

and we have proven here that for
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with norm 1

\iint_{|x|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\leq\frac{a}{2\pi}=\frac{R^{2}}{2\pi}}\mathcal{W}(u,u)(x,\xi)dxd\xi\leq 1-\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\int_{a}^{+{\infty}}e^{-t}t^{n-1}dt=1-\frac{\Gamma(n,R^{2})}{\Gamma(n)},

where the upper incomplete Gamma function
\Gamma(z,x)

is given by

(9.8.8)

\Gamma(z,x)=\int_{x}^{+{\infty}}t^{z-1}e^{-t}dt.

This is indeed the result of Theorem 1 in
[
39
]
.

N.B
.

Let
x>0
be given and let
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\operatorname{Re}z>0
. Then we have

\Gamma(z,x)=\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}(s+x)^{z-1}e^{-s-x}ds=e^{-x}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}(s+x)^{z-1}e^{-s}ds,

so that if
z=n+1
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we find

\Gamma(n+1,x)=e^{-x}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}(s+x)^{n}e^{-s}ds=e^{-x}\sum_{0\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{k}x^{k}\int_{0}^{+{\infty}}s^{n-k}e^{-s}ds\\
=e^{-x}\sum_{0\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{k}x^{k}\Gamma(n+1-k)=n!e^{-x}\sum_{0\leq k\leq n}\frac{x^{k}}{k!}.
